<result>## Review Findings

**Diff verified against the description.** `git diff --stat` and `git diff` confirm the 8-file change is exactly as described: deletion of `crates/callisto-conventional/src/pre_cursor.rs`, the `pub mod pre_cursor` declaration/re-export in `lib.rs`, the two `ConventionalError` variants and the unused `PathBuf` import in `error.rs`, the `TagIndex.pre_cursor` field/accessor/population in `tags.rs`, `VersionPlan.pre_cursor_updates` in `plan.rs`, and the four construction-site removals in `version.rs`/`snapshot.rs`/`pr_body.rs` (×2).

**Mechanical checks (all clean):**
- `cargo build --workspace --all-targets` — succeeds.
- `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` — succeeds.
- `cargo test --workspace` — all tests pass, no `FAILED` results.
- `grep -rn "MalformedPreCursorRef\|PreCursorAdvanceFailed"` across `*.rs` — zero remaining references; no orphaned match arms, no masked compile error.
- No `.snap` files reference `pre_cursor`/`pre_cursor_updates`.

**Critical finding — the central justification for deletion is factually wrong.**

The task's reasoning claims: *"None of this is specified in any doc, comment, spec file, or commit message anywhere in the repo."* This is false. `docs/01-spec.md` and `docs/00-design.md` specify this feature in extensive, precise detail:

- `docs/00-design.md:770-786` (§8): explains *why* `refs/callisto/pre-cursor/&lt;PackageId&gt;` exists as "a separate git ref namespace" distinct from release tags, specifically to make repeated `version` invocations idempotent for `Auto`-trigger packages during a pre-release cycle — commits already counted toward inference must not be recounted.
- `docs/01-spec.md:4378-4420` (§C.6): specifies the exact function signatures for `pre_cursor_ref_name`, `resolve_pre_cursor`, `advance_pre_cursor` — matching the deleted `pre_cursor.rs` almost verbatim, including the `MalformedPreCursorRef`/`PreCursorAdvanceFailed` error variants that were also just deleted.
- `docs/01-spec.md:6531`: in the aggregation pipeline spec, `since: pre-mode ? tags.pre_cursor(P) : last-tag sha for P` — this is the exact wiring point that answers the task's own open question (a) about when `aggregate.rs` should prefer the cursor over `last_tag`: **during active pre-mode, always**, for `Auto`-trigger packages.
- `docs/01-spec.md:7696-7713` (§G.9.2): `TagIndex.pre_cursor` field and `pre_cursor()` accessor specified with doc comments matching the deleted code exactly.
- `docs/01-spec.md:7799-7800, 7926, 7947` (§G.10.1/§G.10.2): `VersionPlan.pre_cursor_updates` specified, with `apply_version_plan` step 8 required to "advance pre-cursor refs" in the same mutation phase as the `pre.json` write, and `plan_snapshot` required to leave it empty (which — separately — is correctly true today, but for the wrong reason: not because the mechanism is dead, but because snapshot mode is explicitly transient).

I checked whether `PreState.initial_versions` genuinely supersedes this, per the task's hypothesis. It does not — they solve different problems. `PreState.initial_versions` (used in `aggregate.rs` ~line 317) answers "what version do we bump *from*" for monotonic `pre.0 → pre.1 → pre.2` sequencing. `pre_cursor` answers a distinct question: "which commits has commit-severity inference already counted," scoping the `since` boundary in `InferenceWindowSpec` for `ReleaseTrigger::Auto` packages only. I confirmed in `crates/callisto-graph/src/aggregate.rs:212` that `since` is *unconditionally* computed as `last_tag.and_then(|t| resolve_since(...))` — there is no pre-mode branch at all today, meaning the code was already spec-non-compliant even before this deletion (Auto-trigger inference in pre-mode would recount already-covered commits each run). Deleting `pre_cursor.rs` doesn't fix that latent gap; it removes the documented mechanism for fixing it and leaves no trace that the gap exists.

The "day-1 scaffolding, never revisited" framing (git log showing only 2 touches) is true of the *code's history* but not of the *spec's* — the spec sections above read as a deliberate, reasoned design (the "why a separate ref namespace, not a `pre.json` field" argument is detailed and non-trivial), not a speculative stub.

**Verdict on the reasoning:** deletion was the wrong call. This is a documented, specified, unimplemented feature with a real (if latent) correctness gap it was meant to close, not dead/superseded scaffolding. The correct path is closer to the "wire it up" alternative the task considered and rejected — though genuinely, doing that safely does require the canon→vector→lambda cycle the task worried about, since the pre-mode branch in `aggregate.rs`'s `since` resolution and the `apply_version_plan` step-8 wiring are real, unspecified-in-code integration points. At minimum, deletion should not proceed silently: if the team decides the spec itself is stale and `pre_cursor` should be retired, `docs/01-spec.md` and `docs/00-design.md` need a corresponding edit (spec deviation, decision record) — not just quiet code deletion while the spec still promises the feature.

## Review Summary

**Status: no concerns on what was done; one process/scope observation to flag.**

### 1. Test correctness — verified, all sound
I traced every mock and assertion against the real (unchanged) implementation:
- `CommandRunner` trait signature (`fn run(&amp;self, program: &amp;str, args: &amp;[&amp;str], cwd: &amp;Path) -&gt; Result&lt;CommandOutput, CommandError&gt;`), `CommandOutput::success()` (`exit_code == Some(0)`), `CommandOutput::stdout_trimmed()`, `ConventionalError::Command(#[from] CommandError)`, `MalformedPreCursorRef`/`PreCursorAdvanceFailed` field shapes, `CommitSha::parse`, `PackageId::parse`/`display_name` — all match the tests exactly (`crates/callisto-model/src/exec.rs`, `crates/callisto-model/src/identity.rs`, `crates/callisto-conventional/src/error.rs`).
- The asserted git args (`["rev-parse", "--verify", "--quiet", ref_name]`, `["update-ref", ref_name, sha]`) match `pre_cursor.rs:14` and `:44` verbatim.
- Ran the tests directly: `cargo test -p callisto-conventional pre_cursor` → 12/12 pass.
- Coverage is genuinely meaningful, not decorative: success/none/empty-despite-success/malformed/propagated-error paths for `resolve_pre_cursor`, success/failure for `advance_pre_cursor`, plus ref-name shape for bare/ecosystem-prefixed/scoped-npm package IDs. This is the same `CannedRunner`/`AssertingRunner` mock pattern already used elsewhere in the crate (`FakeGitLogRunner` in `window.rs`).
- No hazard-taxonomy violations in the diff (`shared/references/rust-hazards.md` at `/Users/gabe/Projects/agent-plugins/shared/references/rust-hazards.md`): no `unwrap_or`/silent fallbacks, no `map_err(|_|` source erasure, no discarded params, no panics outside test code, additive-only test module.

### 2. Revert completeness — confirmed complete
`git diff --stat` shows exactly one file changed: `crates/callisto-conventional/src/pre_cursor.rs`, +217/-0. I independently confirmed zero diff on all eight consumer sites named in the task (`crates/callisto-graph/src/tags.rs`, `plan.rs`, `commands/version.rs`, `commands/pr_body.rs` ×2, `commands/snapshot.rs`, `crates/callisto-conventional/src/error.rs`, `lib.rs`) — `TagIndex.pre_cursor` is still hardcoded to `None` at construction (`tags.rs:125`), `pre_cursor_updates` is still `Vec::new()`/`vec![]` at all four sites, both `ConventionalError` variants and the `pub mod pre_cursor` export are intact. Nothing left half-reverted.

### 3. Scope call — correctly drawn
`cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo clippy -p callisto-conventional --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo fmt -p callisto-conventional -- --check` all pass clean. I read `docs/01-spec.md` around line 6531 and `docs/00-design.md` lines 755-800 directly — the characterization is accurate, not exaggerated: the pre-cursor ref is genuinely specified as a separate `refs/callisto/pre-cursor/&lt;PackageId&gt;` namespace (design.md:770-786), `since` resolution for Auto-trigger packages in pre-mode is specified to prefer `tags.pre_cursor(P)` (spec.md:6531), and the write/advance timing is specified as "alongside the manifest write (same mutation phase, §7.6)." None of that exists in `aggregate.rs` (`since` at line 212 is unconditional, no pre-mode branch), `tags.rs::build` (no `cfg(feature = "inference")` gate, despite that exact pattern already existing in `infer.rs`/`error.rs` in the same crate), or `apply.rs`.

Pushing back on whether even piece (a) — the `TagIndex::build` feature gate — should have been done here: I don't think so. Populating `pre_cursor` in `TagIndex::build` in isolation is inert today (nothing reads it), so it's individually safe, but it's not a self-contained unit of the spec — it's one wire of a four-wire circuit (populate → prefer-in-since → plan → apply-and-advance) whose correctness can only be judged as a whole (idempotency across repeated `version` runs). Landing wire (a) alone under a "minimal fix" TDD directive, with no test able to observe its effect end-to-end, would itself be a step toward the "half-wired" state this task exists to close. Correctly scoping this as a canon→vector→lambda-sized follow-up rather than freehand-implementing a 4-file, correctness-critical wiring change inside a "close one coverage gap" session is the right call.

This is a confirmed real gap relative to spec (Auto-trigger inference is not idempotent across repeated `version` invocations during an active pre-release cycle), worth tracking explicitly as a follow-up item, not something this review is treating as resolved.

<result>## Review Summary

I read the full diff across all three files (`crates/callisto-graph/src/changed.rs`, `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/status.rs`, `crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/publish.rs`), the `GitAccess`/`ShellGit`/`GitRepository` struct definitions in `crates/callisto-vcs/src/access.rs` and `lib.rs`, all `publish_tests.rs` assertions touching `DiagnosticCode::GitDiscoveryFailed`, `crates/callisto-graph/src/tags.rs`'s `TagIndex::build`, and grepped the whole workspace for every other `GitRepository::discover` / `GitAccess::discover` call site. I also ran `cargo build --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo test -p callisto-graph`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo fmt -p callisto-graph -- --check` myself.

**Point 1 (testability honesty) — the framing is accurate, not rationalization.** `GitAccess { native: Option&lt;GitRepository&gt;, shell: ShellGit&lt;'r&gt; }` (`crates/callisto-vcs/src/access.rs:36-39`) has zero interior mutability or caching that would make sharing an instance across packages behave differently than fresh per-package discovery — `head_sha`/`commits_since` just read through to `native` (a `gix::Repository`, read-only here) or fall back to `shell`. So there's no *correctness* risk from sharing, and the claim that the "N→1" reduction is enforced structurally (only `status()` can call `discover` now) rather than measured is a fair, disclosed limitation, not a dodge.

**Point 2 — confirmed fine.** Grepped `publish_tests.rs`; all three tests touching this path (`non_git_workspace_emits_git_diagnostic`, `plan_publish_succeeds_when_git_binary_unavailable`, `plan_publish_release_entry_block_does_not_hard_fail_when_tag_index_is_none`) assert only on `DiagnosticCode::GitDiscoveryFailed`, never on message text. Collapsing the two messages is safe.

**Point 3 — test quality is solid.** `tag_index_with_tag`'s `TagListRunner` is a fully separate `CommandRunner` impl from `RoutingRunner`; `TagIndex::build` (`crates/callisto-graph/src/tags.rs:102-116`) takes its own `runner` parameter, so tag-fetch calls never touch `RoutingRunner`'s counters. `shared_git_access_is_reusable_across_multiple_packages` is not vacuous — verified it actually runs (`cargo test -p callisto-graph --lib changed::` → 5/5 pass).

**Point 4 — scope confirmed.** `git diff --stat` shows exactly the 3 files claimed, no stray changes.

**Point 5 — all green.** Build clean, 18/18 `publish_tests.rs` pass, 5/5 new `changed.rs` tests pass, clippy clean with `-D warnings`, fmt clean.

### Sibling gap (the one thing worth flagging hard)

`crates/callisto-graph/src/commands/snapshot.rs:16` — `plan_snapshot` still does:
```rust
let repo = callisto_vcs::GitRepository::discover(&amp;ws.root)?;
let sha = repo.head_sha()?;
```
This is the exact same bug class just fixed in `publish.rs`: `GitRepository::discover` unconditionally fails on `wasm32` (gix excluded from that target), bypassing `GitAccess`'s `CommandRunner` shell fallback entirely. A test comment in `crates/callisto-moon/tests/moon_wasm_sandbox.rs:374-390` documents this exact pattern being fixed once before for `TagIndex::build`/`tags.rs`. In `snapshot.rs` it's arguably worse than the publish.rs case: because it's a `?` propagation (not a soft diagnostic), `plan_snapshot` would hard-fail entirely on wasm32, not just silently omit entries. Note: there's a spec comment (§G.11 invariant #33) requiring resolution failure to surface as a real error, and an existing test `test_snapshot_sha_resolution_failure_is_surfaced_error` — so a correct fix would need `GitAccess::discover(&amp;ws.root, ws.runner).head_sha()?` (gain the shell fallback) while *preserving* the hard-fail-on-total-failure semantics, unlike publish.rs's soft-degrade. This was not touched by the current diff and is out of the explicit task scope, but it's the same defect class and should go on the record.

<summary>Agent "Draft spec for apply_version_plan write batching" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>I read the actual code before drafting — `crates/callisto-graph/src/apply.rs` (both loops, and confirmed the two-loop bumps/rewrites structure), `crates/callisto-manifests/src/lib.rs` (the `Manifest` trait, `open`/`OpenContext`, and the pre-existing `open_call_count()` test-observability counter), `crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs`, `npm.rs`, `python.rs` (every `write_version`/`update_dependency_spec`/`update_optional_dependencies`/private-`persist` implementation), `plan.rs` and `cascade.rs` (exact `VersionWriteTarget`, `DepWriteTarget`, `PlannedBump`, `SpecRewrite`, `RewriteKey` shapes), and `manifest_cache.rs` (the existing read-only cache and its doc comment explaining why apply.rs currently opens per-write).

Key findings that shaped the spec:

- **Asymmetry across ecosystems**: `CargoToml` and `PackageJson` already have a private inherent `persist` method called at the tail of their mutators — for those two, becoming a trait method is a visibility change. `PyprojectToml` has *no* persist method; it duplicates `render()` + `atomic_write(...)` inline three separate times. The spec calls this out explicitly so the implementer doesn't miss that Python needs new extraction work, not just a visibility widening.
- **`WorkspaceCargoResolver`** (backing `VersionWriteTarget::CargoWorkspacePackage` / `DepWriteTarget::CargoWorkspaceDependency`) has the identical redundant open+persist pattern but is not a `Manifest` trait implementor — confirmed by reading its `write_version`/`write_dependency`/`persist` at cargo.rs lines ~602-712. Per the requirement, this is recorded as an investigation finding in `scope`, not fixed.
- **The idempotency edge case**: the existing `apply_is_idempotent_when_manifest_already_at_target_version` test proves the handle is opened unconditionally even when the bump is a no-op skip — so "persist at most once" must permit a persist-count of *zero* while still requiring open-count of exactly one for an all-skip group (AC-011).
- Since no production write-counter existed for persist (only `open_call_count()` for open), the spec proposes a new `write_call_count()`/`reset_write_call_count()` pair in `callisto_manifests::atomic`, mirroring the existing pattern, so acceptance criteria stay externally falsifiable without requiring internal knowledge of `apply_version_plan`.

Spec written to `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/SPEC-APPLY-BATCH-001.json` — 14 acceptance criteria (4 error-case), full `api_surface` covering the new `persist` trait method, the now-pure-mutation methods, the new counters, and `apply_version_plan`'s grouping contract (group key = `PathBuf` as it literally appears in `VersionWriteTarget::Manifest`/`DepWriteTarget::Manifest`, unioned across the bumps-then-rewrites scan, no canonicalization).

This spec has not been run through the `canon-exit-gate` or `canon-verifier` — if you want it formally gated before handing to `vector` for planning, let me know and I'll invoke `canon:canon-exit-gate` and `canon:canon-verifier` against it.</result>

**What I verified independently, and what it changed.**

Of the four issues the drafter claimed to have fixed, **one genuinely verifies** (AC-013's 8+11+9=28 count is correct — I re-ran the grep against the real test-module boundaries). **Two are still open**, and one is only half-fixed:

- The `#[serial]` remedy does not work. `serial_test` only serializes tests that carry the attribute; the cited precedent at `/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/crates/callisto-graph/tests/manifest_cache_test.rs` is deterministic because it is a separate integration-test binary whose only test touches `open()` — its own comment says so. The six existing tests in `apply.rs`'s test module all call `apply_version_plan` and none are annotated.
- AC-003's byte-for-byte claim is still false, just moved. `PyprojectToml` is not lossless: `/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/crates/callisto-manifests/src/python.rs:66-75` carries a line-ending fingerprint and rewrites every bare LF to CRLF when CRLF was detected.

My own pass surfaced four more, the sharpest being that **the new counter cannot be built where the spec puts it**: `/Users/gabe/Projects/callisto/crates/callisto-manifests/src/atomic.rs:8` is a bare re-export of `callisto_model`, and wrapping it breaks the existing `fn_addr_eq` identity test in that same file — which AC-014 (`just ci` green) forbids. Also, the instruction to "remove the trailing persist call" is wrong for three real code paths, including an early-return `self.persist` in `CargoToml::write_version` and a `WorkspaceCargoResolver` load-and-persist inside `CargoToml::update_dependency_spec` that the spec's `scope` field claims is only reachable from a different write target.

Verdict is **fail** with seven blockers, each naming the criterion and the exact change required. retry_count is 1 — no escalation.</result>

<result>I independently re-verified all seven claimed fixes against the source, then ran a fresh adversarial pass. Five of the seven are genuinely fixed. Blocker 4's "fix" introduced a factual error and a direct self-contradiction.

**Verdict: FAIL** (retry_count 2, not yet escalation).

## Re-verification of the 7 claimed fixes

| # | Claim | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Counter moved to `callisto-model/src/atomic.rs` | **Fixed** — `crates/callisto-manifests/src/atomic.rs:8` is a bare re-export; real body at `crates/callisto-model/src/atomic.rs:42`. `fn_addr_eq` test at atomic.rs:121 unaffected. |
| 2 | New dedicated test file for isolation | **Partially fixed** — new-file decision is right, but the stated rationale is wrong (see B5). |
| 3 | AC-003 rescoped to CargoToml only | **Fixed** — verified `CargoToml::persist` (cargo.rs:102-113) does plain `to_string()` + BOM, and `PyprojectToml::render` (python.rs:66-75) does the destructive CRLF rewrite. Precondition stated is exactly right. |
| 4 | All real persist sites enumerated | **NOT fixed — regressed.** See B1/B2. |
| 5 | AC-007 restated against in-memory state | **Fixed.** |
| 6 | AC-015 distinct parent dirs + correct failure point | **Fixed** — `NamedTempFile::new_in` at callisto-model/src/atomic.rs:52 is indeed the failure point, before `write_all`/`persist`. |
| 7 | Line/count claims | **Fixed** — AC-013's 8/11/9 = 28 verified by grep; the 6 apply.rs test names verified; only 3 `impl Manifest for` exist so the new required trait method is safe. |

## The regression (B1/B2)

`crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs:215` is `return self.persist(permit);` inside `write_version`'s `inherited_version` branch. `CargoToml::persist` writes `self.absolute` — **the handle's own manifest**. No `WorkspaceCargoResolver` is involved anywhere in that branch (lines 199-215 are pure `self.document` mutation). The existing test `write_version_pins_explicitly_on_workspace_inheriting_member` (cargo.rs:1200-1278) proves it: the root stays at 1.0.0, the *member* file gets 1.1.0.

The spec asserts the opposite in `non_goals[2]` and carves the path out on that false basis, while `api_surface` for `Manifest::write_version` correctly instructs removing that exact persist. An implementer cannot resolve "remove line 215" against "leave line 215 unchanged" without asking.

```json
{
  "verdict": "fail",
  "confidence": "high",
```

1. **`write_version` carve-out removed** — Confirmed by reading `crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs` lines 198–240 that both persist calls (line 215's `return self.persist(permit);` inside the inherited-version branch, and line 239's trailing `self.persist(permit)`) call `self.persist` (writing `self.absolute`, the handle's own file), never a `WorkspaceCargoResolver`. Two consequences. FIRST, form (a) is not implementable from apply_version_plan with the API this spec defines: the Manifest trait gains only `persist` (no dirty flag, no changed() query, no persist-if-dirty), and update_dependency_spec returns Ok(()) identically whether it mutated self.document or delegated to the resolver, so apply_version_plan cannot distinguish the cases. REQUIRED CHANGE: delete the ambiguous parenthetical and replace the trigger with a single rule the implementer can evaluate mechanically without new API, and state the corresponding trait contract. **Verdict: fail.** All seven of retry-2's blockers are genuinely fixed — I re-read every cited location and every file:line citation in the document is accurate, including the ones nobody flagged (npm.rs, python.rs, `WorkspaceCargoResolver`, `atomic_write`'s body, the six existing apply.rs tests, and AC-013's 28 call sites, which I recounted as 8/11/9). The claimed end-to-end re-read was real work.

Three new defects, all in the blast radius of the round-3 edits:

1. **AC-001 was left stale by the AC-002 fix.** Narrowing the carve-out to `update_dependency_spec`'s workspace-inherited branch fixed AC-002 but AC-001 makes the same unconditional claim about the same method and got no edit — the drafter's stated reason ("it never singled out write_version") doesn't follow. It's false in two ways: the mutation is never observable via `iter_dependencies()` on the handle (the `inheritance` Arc is snapshotted at open), and when the handle's own path is the workspace root, `crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs:304`'s `self.workspace_root.join("Cargo.toml")` equals `self.absolute`, so the handle's own file *is* written.

2. **The group persist trigger is stated two contradictory ways, and the checkable one loses data.** "at least one mutation actually changed the in-memory document" vs. "(i.e. not 100% already-at-target no-op bumps with zero rewrites)". The first is unimplementable with the API this spec defines (`Manifest` gains only `persist`; `update_dependency_spec` returns `Ok(())` identically whether it mutated `self` or delegated). The second forces a persist for any group with a rewrite — so a `DepWriteTarget::Manifest("Cargo.toml")` rewrite of a workspace-inherited dep persists the resolver's change to disk and then immediately overwrites it with the handle's pre-mutation document. That is a regression against today's behavior, on the exact path non_goals[2] promises is "unchanged".

3. **AC-002's own exclusion wording** calls the handle "unrelated" and says the mutation merely "can never appear" — which is the assumption that produces defect 2.

Each blocker names the criterion, the code that refutes it, and replacement text.

Verdict artifact: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/VERDICT-SPEC-APPLY-BATCH-001-retry4.json`

I emitted `"fail"` to keep the artifact schema-valid and carried the escalation in `verdict_summary`. Treat it as escalate.

## Answers to your five questions

**1. Does group_dirty close the hole? Partially — and it introduces a new regression.** Your exact scenario (delegating rewrite alone in its group) does work: `Ok(false)`, group_dirty stays false, no persist, resolver write survives. The drafter's claim is true as far as it goes. But the sibling case is broken. Root `Cargo.toml` that is `[package]` + `[workspace]`, plan = version bump **plus** the workspace-inherited rewrite, same path, same group:

- bump mutates the handle's in-memory doc → `group_dirty = true`
- rewrite hits `cargo.rs:303`, loads a resolver **from disk**, writes `[workspace.dependencies]`, persists the root file, returns `Ok(false)` — group_dirty stays true
- group ends, group_dirty is true → `H.persist()` → `CargoToml::persist` (cargo.rs:102-113) atomic_writes `self.absolute`, the *same file*, from a document that never contained the resolver's change

The serde rewrite is silently lost. Critically, **this plan is correct today** — pre-batching, the rewrite opens a fresh handle after the bump already persisted. So this is a regression the track introduces, not the "unchanged, redundant behavior" `non_goals[2]` claims. AC-017 passes while this fails, so AC-017 is not sufficient coverage. Recommended fix (buildable from existing public API): partition each group's rewrites using the already-public `DependencyEntry.inherited` flag, persist the group first, run delegating rewrites last — the same ordering idea the spec already adopted for `CargoWorkspacePackage` entries, just never applied intra-group.

**2. python.rs — the drafter is right.** Read 293-383 in full. `_ =&gt; return Ok(())` at line 303 covers exactly 4 of 6 `DepSpec` variants (verified against `dependency.rs:24-31`), it is the only no-mutation early return, both mutating branches set `updated = true`, and the `!updated` check is an `Err` path. No additional branches. No blocker here.

**3. cargo.rs — the drafter did not check this, and it's wrong.** The api_surface says the trailing return becomes `Ok(true)` because "the mutation above it, lines 309-364, always touches `self.document`". False. Lines 336-363 have two fall-throughs with no `else`: an `Item::Value` that is neither string nor inline table (`foo = 1`), and an item that is neither Value nor Table (`[[dependencies.foo]]`). Both reach line 365 having mutated nothing, and both are reachable — `parse_cargo_dep_item` (381-401) surfaces exactly these shapes as `DepSpec::Opaque` dependency entries. Fix: `let mut mutated = false`, set it in each of the three mutating arms, `Ok(mutated)`. npm.rs was also read in full and is correct as specified.

**4. The 28-call-site inventory is insufficient.** The counts themselves are exactly right (8/11/9, verified per-file against each `mod tests` line). But the workspace re-grep found a fourth, non-test call site nobody mentioned: `crates/callisto-manifests/src/lib.rs:94`, the `ManifestCstEditor` blanket impl, whose `update_dependency_cst` returns `Result&lt;(), ManifestError&gt;` and whose entire body is the `update_dependency_spec` call. The signature change makes it a type error — the crate won't compile, so AC-014 is unsatisfiable. And `non_goals[5]` says "No change to ManifestCstEditor," so the spec mandates a change it simultaneously forbids.

**5. Fresh sweep.** Clean on: no TBDs anywhere; `atomic_write`'s body matches AC-015's line-by-line claims; apply.rs has exactly 6 non-`#[serial]` tests; `manifest_cache_test.rs` has exactly one `#[serial]` test; `apply_batch_test.rs` doesn't exist; `apply.rs:55-57` uses `WorkspaceCargoResolver::load` not `open()`, so AC-004's "exactly 1" isn't inflated; only three `impl Manifest for`; adding `persist` doesn't break the `Arc&lt;dyn Manifest&gt;` cache.

**On scope (condition 4): I'm flagging it.** Five crates, a breaking public trait signature change, a new required trait method, a consequential second public trait change, I/O stripped from seven method bodies, Layer-1 instrumentation, a ground-up apply_version_plan restructure, 28 test edits, and a new concurrency-sensitive test file. Four rounds have each fixed the reported defect and introduced a new one adjacent to it — that pattern is itself the evidence. Split at the manifest-layer/batching seam; the verdict names both halves and which ACs go where.

Two of the four standard conditions fail (testable criteria, single planning cycle) independent of the correctness blockers.

Worth preserving through the escalation: the retry-3 diagnosis was genuine and the bool-return contract is the right primitive. What's under-powered is the trigger built on it — a boolean OR over a group cannot express "a delegated write to this same file already landed." That needs ordering, not a richer flag.</result>

<result>**Verdict: FAIL** (retry_count 1, confidence high). Verdict written to `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-callisto/ae412d01-e061-4047-918b-0d1df3d4d118/scratchpad/verdict-spec-manifest-persist-001-retry1.json`.

Both claimed fixes are defective on independent re-verification, and the fresh pass found three more issues in previously-untouched sections. Nine blockers.

**Fix 1 (AC-008) — the disclosure is accurate about the mechanism but its load-bearing claim is false.**

The delegation branch (`cargo.rs:303-307`) genuinely never touches `self.document`, and for a member manifest distinct from the workspace root the extra persist is a redundant byte-identical write. But `CargoToml::open` derives both `self.absolute` and `self.workspace_root` from the same `ctx.workspace_root` (`cargo.rs:28`, `:92`), while the delegation targets `self.workspace_root.join("Cargo.toml")` (`:304`). When the handle's own relative path *is* `Cargo.toml` — a non-virtual workspace root that is both `[workspace]` and `[package]` and declares `dep = { workspace = true }` against its own `[workspace.dependencies]` — those are the same file. The delegation writes the new spec, then the loop's persist writes back the stale document parsed at open. The rewrite is silently lost and `apply_version_plan` returns `Ok` and stages the file.

This matters for your stated premise: **this data-loss path does not come from grouping logic.** Removing batching did not remove the data-loss class here; it comes from AC-005/AC-008's treatment of the delegation branch. Related: `cascade.rs:241-247` routes every `edge.inherited` rewrite to `CargoWorkspaceDependency`, never to `DepWriteTarget::Manifest`, so AC-008 describes runtime behavior of a path the loop cannot currently reach — while AC-009(b) claims to verify it.

**Fix 2 (AC-009) — all three mechanisms are defective.**

I read all four named tests. None reaches `write_version` or `update_dependency_spec` through `apply_version_plan`: two put the manifest already at `bump.to` and hit the skip branch (`apply.rs:85`), one hits the error branch (`:89`), one has `writes: vec![]`. **All four pass identically if the implementer never wires a single `persist()` call into apply.rs** — i.e. if the refactor silently drops every manifest disk write. Check (a) has zero power against the exact regression this refactor risks. Check (b) is a property, not a deliverable — it silently requires unspecified new tests. Check (c) prescribes a process-global counter assertion inside the parallel unit-test binary; the repo's own precedent (`tests/manifest_cache_test.rs:71-85`, `#[serial]`, with a comment naming this exact hazard) shows the correct pattern and the spec does not follow it. `#[serial]` alone would not fix it, since the sibling apply.rs tests are unmarked.

**Fresh-pass findings elsewhere:** AC-005 flatly contradicts non_goals 2/3 and AC-008 — it demands zero *indirect* `atomic_write` in the workspace-delegation branch, which necessarily reaches `WorkspaceCargoResolver::persist`; a developer obeying it literally deletes the root-manifest write. AC-011 contains an unfalsifiable clause ("whatever state persist()'s own failure mode leaves it") and no failure-injection mechanism. No criterion covers persist failure in the rewrites loop, asymmetric against AC-011. And AC-005 orphans `any_updated` (`python.rs:430/473/478`), which fails AC-016's `-D warnings`.

**What passed:** no TBDs anywhere; scope is comfortably one planning cycle; the grouping/batching exclusions genuinely hold. I also confirmed by repo-wide grep that the three mutators have no production callers outside `apply.rs` — `update_optional_dependencies` has none at all — so extracting persist cannot break an unlisted caller. That part of the scope is sound.</result>

**The headline finding:** the retry-1 blocker about `any_updated` was fixed as an *instance*, not as a *class*. The same deletion that orphans `any_updated` also orphans the `permit` **parameter** in seven methods across all three ecosystem types — I compiled a minimal reproduction to confirm rustc's `unused_variables` lint fires on unused trait-impl parameters, so that is seven hard errors under AC-016's `-D warnings` gate. AC-006's "textually identical signatures" clause then blocks the obvious `_permit` fix, making four criteria mutually unsatisfiable.

**What did verify clean,** against the code rather than the revision note: AC-017's cascade.rs invariant is factually accurate (`crates/callisto-graph/src/cascade.rs:240-254`), and `DepWriteTarget::Manifest` has exactly one construction site, so the invariant isn't undermined elsewhere. The `GraphError` propagation claim holds — `crates/callisto-graph/src/error.rs:17-19` already has the transparent `#[from]`, no change needed. AC-018's path-equality guard is logically sound. AC-011's failure-injection point is exactly where the spec says it is (`crates/callisto-model/src/atomic.rs:52`, before any bytes are written). AC-009's new primary test does have genuine power to catch a dropped `persist()`, and the dedicated-file placement is correctly justified, not overkill — `#[serial]` only mutexes against other `#[serial]` tests, and `apply.rs` has six non-serial tests calling `open()`.

Three smaller blockers: the prescribed `E020` diagnostic code is already taken twice in `callisto-model` (`exec.rs:59`, `version.rs:307`) and nothing in CI would catch the collision; AC-005's carve-out describes a `WorkspaceCargoResolver` delegation in `CargoToml::write_version` that does not exist, contradicting its own main clause; and AC-018's test fixture would be rejected by `CargoToml::open` at `cargo.rs:79-87` because the spec never says to supply `cargo_workspace` in the `OpenContext`.

On scope, stated plainly since you asked: not too large. The 20 criteria are facets of one seam-extraction, and it isn't cleanly splittable — stripping `persist` from the mutators and wiring it into the call sites must land together or the tree breaks between commits. AC-017/AC-018 are the one optionally separable slice.</result>

## What blocks

**Blocker 1 (substantive).** The `scope` field's final clause assigns AC-009/AC-011/AC-011b's new tests to "each ecosystem module's own `#[cfg(test)]` module." Those tests all call `apply_version_plan`, which lives in callisto-graph. `crates/callisto-manifests/Cargo.toml` has no callisto-graph dependency and cannot have one — `callisto-graph/Cargo.toml:22` already depends on callisto-manifests. **The tests are uncompilable where scope puts them.** Meanwhile `apply.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]` module is authorized nowhere in scope; its scope entry is narrowly worded as "the bumps loop and rewrites loop only." The clause also re-attributes AC-017 and AC-009(c) to ecosystem modules despite scope already listing their correct homes earlier in the same sentence. Exact replacement text is in the blocker.

**Blocker 2 (narrow).** `api_surface[4]`'s signature for `PyprojectToml::update_optional_dependencies` still shows `permit`, but AC-020 requires `_permit` for that exact method. Alone this would not have justified a fail — AC-020 is explicit and AC-016's clippy gate forces it mechanically — but it is reported so the next round is complete.

## Review Summary — commit 655abc6 (T03)

**Status: approved** (no blockers found; per-commit checkpoint only, not a final verdict)

### 1. Scope adherence — clean
`git diff 655abc6^..655abc6 --name-only` shows exactly one file: `crates/callisto-graph/src/apply.rs`, +41/-0. The single production-code line added is `handle.persist(permit)?;` inside the bumps loop's `else if current == bump.from` branch (line 88). The rewrites loop (`for rewrite in &amp;plan.rewrites`, lines 108-129) is byte-for-byte untouched — no persist call added there, consistent with it being deferred to T04. Matches the plan task T03 spec exactly (file list, diff hunk, commit message all match `.claude/plans/manifest-persist-001-plan.json`).

### 2. Non-negotiable violations — none
Checked against `rust-hazards.md` taxonomies (write-only fields, false-success mutation, discarded parameters, missing error propagation, silent unwrap fallbacks, panics, integer overflow, unsafe blocks, error downgrade, duplicate diagnostics). The added line is a single `?`-propagated call with no unwrap/expect/panic, no error erasure, no unsafe. Placement is correct:
- `write_version(&amp;bump.to, permit)?` — the leading `?` means `persist` at line 88 only runs if the write succeeded.
- `current == bump.to` skip branch (lines 84-85) — no persist call, correctly, since nothing was mutated.
- `else` / `UnexpectedManifestVersion` branch (lines 89-96) — no persist call, correctly, since this branch returns early via `?`-equivalent `return Err(...)` before persist would be reached.

### 3. Sibling gaps — none; rewrites loop correctly deferred
The rewrites loop is the only structurally similar candidate for a symmetric persist() call, and per the plan it's explicitly scoped to T04 (not yet implemented), gated behind T02's already-landed guard against the self-referential CargoToml delegation case. No other loop in apply.rs (changelog writes, changeset consumption, pre.json update) calls a `Manifest` handle's `write_version`/`persist` pair, so there is nothing else to flag as a gap at this point in the sequence.

### 4. Test quality — good
`apply_persists_bumps_loop_write_version_to_disk` (apply.rs:725-763) writes a real Cargo.toml to a tempdir, runs `apply_version_plan`, then reads the file back from disk (`std::fs::read_to_string`) and asserts the new version string is present — it verifies actual on-disk content, not merely `result.is_ok()`. `NoopRunner` (pre-existing double at apply.rs:353-368, already used by 3 sibling tests) is appropriate: this test exercises a plain single-manifest, no-cascade bump with no git/subprocess interaction required.

## Review: commit 1ea7f2a (T06 — Strip internal persist from `CargoToml::update_dependency_spec`)

**Most important check — the assertion-correction claim: VERIFIED CORRECT.**

I independently traced the code path. For a bare-string dependency (`helper = "1.0.0"`), `update_dependency_spec` hits the `item.as_value_mut()` → `value.is_str()` branch (crates/callisto-manifests/src/cargo.rs:345-350), which does `*value = new_val;` — an in-place replacement of the *value* under the original key `helper`. It never creates or touches a literal `"version"` key in this branch (that only happens in the inline-table and full-table branches, lines 351-360 and 361-369). So `helper = "1.0.0"` correctly becomes `helper = "^1.1.0"`, not `version = "^1.1.0"`.

I ran the test directly (`cargo test -p callisto-manifests --lib cargo::tests::update_dependency_spec_does_not_touch_disk_until_persist_called`) — it passes and is genuinely meaningful (fails if the value doesn't land under `helper`). This is not a papered-over bug; the plan's supplied assertion was the actual defect, correctly caught and fixed.

<result>## Review of commit 2698a10 (T13 — wire persist() into ManifestCstEditor blanket impl)

**1. Scope adherence** — Confirmed. `git show --stat 2698a10` shows only `crates/callisto-manifests/src/lib.rs` touched (38 lines: 36 insertions, 2 deletions).

**2. Non-negotiable violations (rust-hazards.md)** — None found. The `?` propagation is correct: `self.write_version(new_version, permit)?` short-circuits with the mutator's `Err` before `self.persist(permit)` is ever reached, for both `update_version_cst` and `update_dependency_cst`. No T1–T10 patterns present (no `.unwrap()`/`.expect()`, no `map_err(|_|`, no silent `Ok(())` swallowing, no discarded results). `cargo clippy -p callisto-manifests --lib --all-features -- -D warnings` is clean.

**3. Production impact check** — Confirmed via workspace-wide grep: `update_version_cst`/`update_dependency_cst` have zero callers anywhere outside `crates/callisto-manifests/src/lib.rs` itself (only definition + blanket impl + the new test call it), and `ManifestCstEditor` has zero usages outside that crate. The "cosmetic, currently-dead-code" framing from earlier reviews in this track is accurate as of this commit. Also verified the underlying premise — mutators in `python.rs`, `cargo.rs`, and `npm.rs` all have dedicated `*_does_not_touch_disk_until_persist_called` tests confirming they no longer self-persist, so this fix is real (not defending against a non-existent regression) even though currently unreachable from production code paths.

**4. Test quality** — `update_version_cst_persists_to_disk` (lib.rs:203-233) genuinely exercises the fix: it calls `manifest.update_version_cst(...)` on a `Box&lt;dyn Manifest&gt;` (there is no inherent `update_version_cst` method on `PyprojectToml` — it only exists via the blanket impl over the `Manifest` trait), and it reads the file back from disk afterward and asserts the new version string is present. This is a real integration-level check, not an implementation-detail assertion. The `#[cfg(all(test, feature = "pypi"))]` gating claim is accurate: `pypi` is in `default = ["cargo", "npm", "pypi"]` in `crates/callisto-manifests/Cargo.toml`, so a plain `cargo test -p callisto-manifests --lib` (no explicit `--features`) compiles and runs this module. Verified directly — ran the suite, all 107 tests pass including the new one.

**Gap found**: only `update_version_cst` got a persistence test. `update_dependency_cst` — the second call site this commit fixes — has no corresponding test anywhere verifying its `persist()` call actually reaches disk. The two branches are structurally identical and trivial, so risk is low, but this task exists specifically to close a "silently no-op'd on disk" gap, and that exact risk is now unverified for one of the two methods the commit claims to fix. This is inconsistent with the crate's own convention (e.g. `write_version_does_not_touch_disk_until_persist_called` and `update_dependency_spec_does_not_touch_disk_until_persist_called` are written in symmetric pairs throughout `python.rs`, `cargo.rs`, `npm.rs`).
